# Tasks

Each task is sized for one fresh Claude Code session. Start a session with: *"Do task T1.2 from TASKS.md."*

Every milestone is broken down into tasks (M3–M10 on 2026-10-06). The later milestones' tasks are coarser: when a milestone is reached, a session first refines its tasks with what the earlier ones taught (that is a task in itself), splitting any task too big for one session. Milestone definitions are in SPEC §11.

Legend: `[ ]` todo, `[x]` done.

## M0 — Project skeleton

- [x] **T0.1 Toolchain + Godot project.** Install, or document how to install, the latest stable Godot 4.x .NET build and the .NET SDK it requires, on Linux. Create the Godot C# project with the folder layout from SPEC §4.1. Split `Sim` and `Wad`/`Map` into plain .NET class libraries (`net10.0`, no Godot reference) referenced by the Godot project, so the sim's purity is enforced by the compiler. Record the pinned versions in SPEC §12.
  *Done when:* the project opens in Godot, runs an empty main scene, and `dotnet build` succeeds from the CLI.
- [x] **T0.2 Test setup.** Add an xUnit test project for the plain libraries, with a helper that locates `wads/DOOM1.WAD` and skips a test when the file is absent. Add a test that scans the `Sim` assembly and fails if it uses `float`, `double`, `System.Random` or `DateTime` (SPEC §6.1).
  *Done when:* `dotnet test` runs green and the purity test fails when a float is temporarily introduced in `Sim`.
- [ ] **T0.3 CI + commands.** Add a GitHub Actions workflow that runs build and tests, plus a headless Godot export for Linux x86_64. Add a `## Commands` section to `AGENTS.md` listing build, test, run and export.
  *Done when:* CI is green on a pushed branch.
  *Status:* workflow, export preset and `## Commands` added (branch `t0.3-ci`); build, test, headless export and the exported-binary smoke test pass locally, and `actionlint` is clean. Not ticked: the repo has no remote yet, so CI has not run. Tick after the first push shows a green run.
- [ ] **T0.4 Interactive editor check.** Open the project in the Godot editor with a real display (Wayland/X11 passthrough in the dev container) and run the main scene with F5; T0.1 only verified this headlessly. Fix any GPU/display issues in `.devcontainer/`.
  *Done when:* the editor opens without errors and the main scene runs in a window with the Forward+ renderer.

## M1 — WAD viewer

- [x] **T1.1 WAD reader.** Parse the header and lump directory; look lumps up by name; handle namespace markers (`S_`, `F_`, `P_` and their `SS_`/`FF_` variants); merge PWADs over the IWAD. SPEC §5.1.
  *Done when:* tests against `DOOM1.WAD` assert the lump count, the `IWAD` type, and that `E1M1`, `PLAYPAL` and `TROOA1` are found.
- [x] **T1.1a IWAD location.** Find the IWAD through a configurable path, the game folder and common install locations (Steam/GOG), with a file picker as the fallback, and load PWADs given on the command line (`-file`) through `WadArchive`. SPEC §5.1.
  *Done when:* the game starts with `DOOM1.WAD` from each search location in turn, and shows the picker when none is found.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Wad.IwadLocator` (d_iwad.c port, unit-tested on temp folders with a faked environment) searches `-iwad`, `ISODOOM_IWAD`/`ISODOOM_DOOM1_WAD`, the configured path (`user://settings.cfg`, written by the picker), the working directory, the game folder and its `wads/`, `DOOMWADDIR`/`DOOMWADPATH`, XDG folders, Steam (all libraries) and GOG folders/registry keys; `-file` PWADs load through `WadArchive`. The editor run and the exported binary both started with `DOOM1.WAD` from each Linux location, and the viewer opens the file picker when nothing is found (checked with a real display). The loading path is `WadViewer.LoadWad` for now. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T1.1b Optional shareware download.** SPEC §5.1: when no IWAD is found, offer to download the shareware `DOOM1.WAD` (v1.9, MD5 `f0cefca49926d00903cf57551d901abe`) next to the picker, if a stable, redistributable source exists and it is cheap (SPEC §12: otherwise deferred). Check the shareware licence terms first, verify the MD5, and save it to the game folder or `user://` and the configured path.
  *Done when:* with no IWAD anywhere, the download option fetches and verifies `DOOM1.WAD` and the game starts with it, or the task is closed with the reason logged in SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T1.1c IWAD search on real installs.** T1.1a's Windows (GOG/Steam registry keys, GOG Galaxy folder), macOS and Steam Deck paths are only unit-tested with faked folders. Check them against real installs (Steam Ultimate Doom/Doom II including the 2024 re-release folders, a GOG install, a Steam Deck SD-card library) and fix any folder names.
  *Done when:* the game finds the IWAD unaided on at least a Windows Steam or GOG install and a Steam Deck.
- [x] **T1.2 IWAD identification.** Detect shareware, registered, Ultimate, Doom II and Final Doom from the lumps present, as vanilla `IdentifyVersion` does. SPEC §2.
  *Done when:* `DOOM1.WAD` is detected as shareware, and the other modes are covered by synthetic test WADs.
- [x] **T1.2a Modified-game checks and IWAD variants.** Vanilla `D_DoomMain` (d_main.c) refuses PWADs with the shareware IWAD ("You cannot -file with the shareware version. Register!") and checks that a registered IWAD holds the episode 2-3 lumps. Decide whether to port the shareware refusal as vanilla behaviour (with a tweak flag to allow PWADs, SPEC §6.3) or drop it, and wire the check into the loading path from T1.1a. Also detect Chocolate Doom's `gamevariant` (Freedoom via the `FREEDOOM` lump, FreeDM via `FREEDM`, BFG Edition via `DMENUPIC`) on top of `IwadInfo`. SPEC §2.
  *Done when:* tests on synthetic WADs cover the chosen PWAD policy and each variant.
  *Done:* vanilla policy kept, with no override: `ModifiedGame.D_CheckModifiedGame` (d_main.c) refuses PWADs with the shareware IWAD and rejects a "registered" IWAD missing vanilla's 23 lumps when PWADs are loaded; Freedoom/FreeDM skip both checks (Chocolate Doom). `WadViewer.LoadWad` runs it after `D_IdentifyVersion`. `IwadInfo.GameVariant` (`vanilla`, `freedoom`, `freedm`, `bfgedition`) comes from the IWAD's own lumps. Real-WAD tests: DOOM1 refuses a PWAD, DOOM II (`ISODOOM_DOOM2_WAD` or `wads/doom2.wad`) is `commercial`/`doom2`/`vanilla` and accepts one. See SPEC §12.
- [x] **T1.3 Palette, colormap and graphics decoders.** Decode `PLAYPAL`, `COLORMAP`, and patch-format graphics (column posts) into 8-bit index buffers plus offsets. Decode flats. SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* unit tests pass on known lump sizes and offsets, and a debug export of `TITLEPIC` and `TROOA1` to PNG looks right.
- [x] **T1.3a Tall patches (PWAD graphics).** `Patch.Decode` places posts at their absolute `topdelta`, as vanilla does, so patches taller than 254 pixels can't be expressed. Many PWADs use the DeePsea "tall patch" convention (a `topdelta` not greater than the previous post's is relative to it), which Chocolate Doom ignores but most source ports support. Decide whether to support it (it changes nothing for IWAD graphics) and log the choice in SPEC §12.
  *Done when:* a synthetic tall-patch test decodes to the chosen layout.
  *Note (from T1.4):* `Textures.DrawColumnInCache` walks the raw patch posts with absolute `topdelta` too; if tall patches are supported, change it to match.
  *Done:* supported by default (`PatchTopDeltaMode.Tall`) in `Patch.Decode`, `Patch.IsPatch` and `Textures` composition, with `PatchTopDeltaMode.Vanilla` for absolute `topdelta`s; DOOM1 and Doom II graphics decode and composite identically in both modes. See SPEC §12.
- [x] **T1.4 Wall texture composition.** Parse `PNAMES` and `TEXTURE1` (and `TEXTURE2` when present) and composite patches into textures, including vanilla quirks such as patches that run past the texture's edges.
  *Done when:* `STARTAN3`, `DOOR3` and the masked `MIDGRATE` are visually checked in a PNG export.
  *Note:* `MIDGRATE` is not in the shareware WAD (it is a registered/Doom II texture), so the shareware masked grates `BRNSMALC` and `BRNBIGC` were checked instead; see T1.4a.
- [x] **T1.4a Full-IWAD texture check.** With a registered/Ultimate Doom or Doom II IWAD (not in the repo; point a test at it with an environment variable like `ISODOOM_DOOM1_WAD`), check that every texture in TEXTURE1/TEXTURE2 composites, and visually check `MIDGRATE` and a few multi-patch masked midtextures in the `Doom1TextureTests.ExportDebugPngs`-style export. SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* the full-IWAD tests pass (skipping without the IWAD) and `MIDGRATE` shows transparent holes and no seams.
  *Done:* `Doom2TextureTests` (Doom II, `TestWads.RequireDoom2()`, any version; v1.666-specific counts and lists only for its MD5) composites all 428 textures in both composite modes; `MIDGRATE` (one 128×128 patch, `M1_1`) equals its patch hole for hole and tiles without seams in the checkerboard export (`DOOM2_TEX_*.png`). Doom II has no TEXTURE2, no masked flags and **no multi-patch masked midtexture** (all 11 see-through midtextures are single-patch), so multi-patch composition was checked on solid textures instead: every multi-patch texture is fully opaque except `SKINEDGE`, whose one hole is a missing pixel in patch `HELL8_1` (`SKY2` and `ZZZFACE3` have the same in their single patches). See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T1.4b Registered/Ultimate Doom texture check.** Doom II has no `TEXTURE2`, so real `TEXTURE2` data is only covered by the synthetic tests. With an Ultimate Doom (or registered) IWAD (environment variable, as for Doom II), check that every TEXTURE1/TEXTURE2 texture composites, and export a few of its multi-patch textures. SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* the Doom 1 full-IWAD tests pass (skipping without the IWAD).
- [x] **T1.5 Sprite indexing.** Group sprite lumps into sprite, frame and rotation sets, including mirrored pairs (`TROOA2A8`) and rotation 0. SPEC §5.2, §7.5.

SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* the full-IWAD tests pass (skipping without the IWAD).

Every slot's lump name matches its frame, rotation and flip, Doom II-only sprites (`VILE`, `CYBR`, `SPID`, `SSWV`, `KEEN`, `BBRN`, …) have vanilla's frame counts and rotating frames, and every frame decodes. Nothing in the name table or the indexer needed fixing. See SPEC §12.

SPEC §5.2.

## M2 — Static level render

**M2 is done (T2.9, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** E1M1 renders as a 3D mesh with the palette shader, its textures, alignment and light levels match vanilla's renderer pixel for pixel up to sub-texel sampling and vanilla's own fixed-point wobble (T2.9), and the free-fly debug camera works (T2.7, checked by script). Optional M2 tasks still open, none needed for the criterion: T2.1a (PWAD map robustness), T2.2a (node artefacts in the floor), T2.3a (missing textures; E1M1 has none), T2.6a (pixel-compare diagonal walls in `--level-check`; T2.9 compared them with vanilla), T2.7a (interactive free-fly check, needs a person), T2.9a (frame time on a GPU, needs a GPU), T2.9b (a dotted seam in E1M4).

- [x] **T2.1 Map lump parsing.** Parse `THINGS`, `LINEDEFS`, `SIDEDEFS`, `VERTEXES`, `SEGS`, `SSECTORS`, `NODES`, `SECTORS`, `BLOCKMAP` and `REJECT` into fixed-point structures in `Map/`.
  *Done when:* tests assert the E1M1 element counts and the player 1 start position.
  *Note (from T1.6b):* the synthetic CI IWAD (`tools/SyntheticIwad`) has a structurally valid one-room `E1M1` (4 lines, 1 sector, 1 subsector, no nodes, player 1 start and a barrel). Extend it as needed (a map with real BSP nodes) so map parsing and the level renderer can be checked in CI too, as the viewer is.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.Level.Load` (p_setup.c `P_SetupLevel`'s loaders, Chocolate Doom's order) parses all ten lumps into fixed-point `Vertex`/`Sector`/`Side`/`Line`/`Seg`/`Subsector`/`Node` objects (vanilla's pointers as references), `MapThing`s, a `Blockmap` and a `Reject` (short lumps padded as Chocolate Doom's `PadRejectArray`), runs `P_GroupLines`, and ports `R_PointOnSide`/`R_PointInSubsector`, `m_fixed` and `m_bbox`. `Doom1MapTests` assert the E1M1 counts (138 things, 475 lines, 648 sides, 467 vertexes, 732 segs, 237 subsectors, 236 nodes, 85 sectors) and the player 1 start (1056, −3616, 90°, sector 38); every E1 and Doom II map loads and passes structural checks (`MapTestChecks`). The synthetic `E1M1` is now two rooms (two sectors, a two-sided line with upper/lower textures, three subsectors under two nodes, one partition with no linedef on it) with a real blockmap, checked by `SyntheticMapTests` and `SyntheticIwad.Check`. `IsoDoom.Map` is now under the determinism scan too. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T2.1a PWAD map robustness.** `Level.Load` throws `WadFormatException` on what vanilla would crash on or misread, and supports only vanilla-format maps. For PWADs, consider: Chocolate Doom's `GetSectorAtNullAddress` emulation for two-sided lines with no back side (now: no back sector), building a blockmap when `BLOCKMAP` is missing or too large (`P_CreateBlockMap`, as PrBoom+ does), linedefs with no front side, and a clear refusal of Hexen-format maps and ZDBSP extended/GL nodes (`XNOD`, `ZNOD`, `GL_*` lumps).
  *Done when:* a PWAD test map with each case loads or fails with a clear message, as decided and logged in SPEC §12.
- [x] **T2.2 Subsector polygons.** Build convex subsector polygons by clipping against the BSP partition lines, then group them per sector. SPEC §7.2.
  *Done when:* for every map in E1, the polygon area per sector matches the sector area within tolerance, and there are no gaps in a debug 2D render.
  *Note (from T2.1):* `Level` has everything this needs: `Nodes` (root last, `Children` with `Node.NF_SUBSECTOR`, partition `X`/`Y`/`Dx`/`Dy` in fixed_t), `Subsectors` (`FirstLine`/`NumLines` into `Segs`, `Sector`) and `R_PointInSubsector` for spot checks. The synthetic `E1M1` (runs in CI) has subsectors whose polygon has an edge with no seg on it (node 0's partition at y = 0), so the clipped polygons can be checked there without a WAD: west room 256×256, east room halves 256×128 each. `IsoDoom.Map` is under the Sim determinism scan (`MapPurityTests`): float polygon code must go elsewhere (e.g. `Render/`), or the scan must exclude it (SPEC §12).
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.SubsectorPolygons.Build(level)` walks the node tree from a box around the map, splitting it along each partition (front part to child 0, back to child 1), and clips each leaf to the front of its segs, skipping a seg that runs within 2 units of an edge the cell already has (rounded split vertices otherwise leave long sliver gaps, as in E1M8 and E1M1). Integer only (`Int128` side tests, corners rounded to fixed_t), so it stays in `IsoDoom.Map` under the determinism scan. Output: one clockwise convex polygon per subsector (`PolygonVertex`, fixed_t) and the subsectors per sector. `SubsectorPolygonTests` (with `PolygonChecks`) check the synthetic map exactly (in CI), and for every E1 map (and every Doom II map when present): convex shapes, `R_PointInSubsector` agrees at every centroid, each sector's area matches its linedef area within 16 + 0.75 × its boundary length, and a scanline render (1 sample per map unit for E1, PNGs in `TestResults/maps/`, checked by eye) has no gaps or overlaps. The only exceptions are node artefacts, where a BSP leaf really covers void or another sector (E1M3 subsector 44, E1M6 subsector 227, E1M7 subsector 248, pinned for DOOM1 v1.9), and sectors that do not close. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T2.2a Node artefacts in the floor.** The polygons follow the node tree, so a leaf that covers void or another sector draws a floor there: in DOOM1 v1.9, E1M3 subsector 44 (about 24,600 square units of sector 7 floor in the void west of line 927, because line 933 overshoots the corner by 8 units) and E1M6 subsector 227 (a sector 104 sliver reaching 128 units into sector 20); in Doom II, large ones around sectors that do not close in MAP16, MAP18, MAP21 and MAP30, which may be deliberate tricks. Vanilla never shows the void ones from eye level, but a top-down camera can. Decide whether the floor mesh hides polygons (or parts) the linedefs put in the void, and whether a polygon in another sector keeps its subsector's floor (what the sim uses) or takes the linedef sector's. Rendering only, so behind a presentation option rather than a sim tweak; keep the BSP polygons available.
  *Done when:* the decision is logged in SPEC §12 and the E1M3/E1M6 cases render as decided in the debug render (`PolygonChecks.Render`).
  *Note (from T2.4):* apply the decision through `FloorTriangles.Build`'s `floorSector` argument (return the sector to draw a subsector's floor in, or null to hide it); hidden polygons still give their corners to the T-junction pass. Hiding only part of a polygon would need clipping before `FloorTriangles` (keep the BSP polygons available).

*Order of the remaining M2 work:* T2.3 and T2.4 (plain C#, either order) → T2.5 → T2.6 → T2.7 → T2.8 → T2.9. T2.3–T2.9 together meet the M2 exit criterion (SPEC §11). T2.1a, T2.2a and T2.3a are optional for it (E1M1 has no node artefact and no missing texture); do T2.2a before T2.4 if possible, or apply it afterwards through T2.4's hook. Tasks with CI checks against the synthetic IWAD: T2.3 and T2.4 (unit tests on the synthetic `E1M1`, which `dotnet test` runs in CI), T2.6 (adds `--level-check` to `ci.yml`) and T2.8 (extends it).

- [x] **T2.3 Wall sections.** Build the wall list of SPEC §7.2 from a `Level` as plain C# (integer/fixed_t only, so it can sit in `IsoDoom.Map` under the determinism scan like `SubsectorPolygons`; e.g. `WallSections.Build(level, textures)`), porting the section and pegging rules of r_segs.c `R_StoreWallRange` per linedef side (not per seg, so the BSP's seg splits add no extra vertices): one-sided → middle from floor to ceiling; two-sided → upper where the back ceiling is lower, lower where the back floor is higher, and the masked middle (emitted now, drawn in M3; vanilla draws it once, clipped to the opening, not tiled vertically). Each section keeps its line, side, kind, the two vertexes, its texture number (`Textures.R_TextureNumForName`), the horizontal texture offset (`textureoffset`), and its **bottom, top and vertical texture anchor as references to sector planes plus a constant** (e.g. "back floor", "front ceiling + rowoffset − texture height"), not as numbers, so T2.5's vertex shader can move doors and lifts without a rebuild (SPEC §7.2 *Moving sectors*). Port the sky rule (`F_SKY1` ceiling on both sides: no upper; vanilla's `worldtop = worldhigh`). A required texture that is `-` (vanilla's hall-of-mirrors) is reported in the result, not thrown. Extend the synthetic `E1M1` (`tools/SyntheticIwad`, `SyntheticIwad.Check`) as needed so CI covers each case: upper/lower unpegged, a sky ceiling on both sides of a line, a closed door (zero-height opening), a masked midtexture.
  *Done when:* unit tests check every synthetic section exactly (planes, anchors, offsets: `PANEL` upper/lower, `BRKPNL` x offset 32, `BRICK1` y offset 8 and the new cases) and spot-check E1M1 lines with each pegging flag against hand-worked vanilla values, and every E1 map (and Doom II map, when present) builds with only the known missing-texture sides reported.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.WallSections.Build(level, textures)` ports `R_StoreWallRange`'s section and pegging rules (and `R_RenderMaskedSegRange`'s for masked middles) per linedef side that has a seg, integer only, under the determinism scan. Each `WallSection` has its line, side, sidedef, front/back sector (the seg rule: a back sector only with `ML_TWOSIDED`), kind (`Middle`, `Upper`, `Lower`, `MaskedMiddle`), `V1`/`V2` in the side's direction, texture number (0 = none, as vanilla's `-`), texture height, `textureoffset`, and `Bottom`/`Top`/`TextureTop` as `PlaneRef`s (`FrontFloor`, `FrontCeiling`, `BackFloor`, `BackCeiling`, and for masked middles `HigherFloor`/`LowerCeiling`, plus a fixed_t constant); `Span()` evaluates the drawn span for the current heights. Two-sided sides always get an upper (unless both ceilings are `F_SKY1`) and a lower, even with no height at load, so lifts and rising stairs need no rebuild. `-` where vanilla draws something at load is listed in `Missing`; an unknown texture name throws `WadFormatException` (vanilla's `I_Error`). - [x] **T2.4 Floor triangles.** Turn `SubsectorPolygons` into per-sector floor triangles as plain C# (integer, next to T2.3), SPEC §7.2 *Floors*. Fan-triangulate each polygon, grouped per sector (`BySector`), skipping subsectors whose clipping left an empty polygon. Remove T-junctions: neighbouring polygons share edges but not always corners (a corner of one may sit in the middle of the other's edge), which shows single-pixel cracks on the GPU, so insert the neighbours' corners that lie on a polygon's edges before triangulating (a fan over a convex polygon with collinear extra points still works if it starts at a real corner; drop zero-area triangles). Keep the output winding documented: the polygons are clockwise in map space (y up), and Godot's −Z is usually north, so T2.5 must check the front face after its axis flip. If T2.2a has been decided by then, apply its presentation option here; otherwise leave a hook for it.
  *Done when:* unit tests show, for the synthetic map exactly and for every E1 map (Doom II when present), that each sector's triangle area equals its polygon area, no triangle is degenerate, and no polygon corner lies strictly inside another subsector's edge (no T-junctions left).
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.FloorTriangles.Build(level, polygons, floorSector?)` (integer only, under the determinism scan) welds polygon corners that differ only by rounding (within 1/32 unit, `WeldEpsilon`; neighbours round a shared corner separately), inserts every other polygon's corner that lies on an edge (within 1/256 unit, strictly between its ends; `Rings`), and triangulates each ring by ear clipping (a fan from the first corner when nothing was inserted; never a zero-area or sliver triangle, never a corner left on a diagonal). Output per sector (`BySector`): `SectorFloor` with the subsectors, the distinct corners and a triangle index list, every triangle clockwise in map space (y up). `floorSector` is the T2.2a hook (which sector's floor a subsector draws, or null to hide it; default `Subsector.Sector`). `FloorTriangleTests` (with `FloorChecks`) check the synthetic `E1M1` exactly (15 triangles; the east room's split corner (128, 0) inserted into the west room; areas equal to polygon and linedef areas), the hook, and for every E1 and Doom II map: no degenerate triangle, triangle area equal to the rings' area exactly and to the polygons' within the welding bound, every corner used, no polygon collapsed, and no T-junction anywhere in the map's triangles (E1M1: 692 triangles, 239 corners inserted). See SPEC §12.

Flats are 64×64 tiles in world space; walls use T2.3's offsets. Upload wall textures and flats as RG8 index textures (`IndexedTextures`). Light is flat for now: `colormap = 31 − light/8`, as the viewer's slider (T2.8 does the real mapping). Use a fixed overview camera framing the map (T2.7 adds the free-fly one). **Design decisions to make and log in SPEC §12:** (1) how textures are bound (a material per texture, a texture array, or an atlas, with repeat wrapping in mind); (2) tiling: vanilla tiles horizontally by `texturewidthmask` (the largest power of two not above the width) and vertically by 128 (`R_DrawColumn`'s `& 127`), so a texture whose height is not 128 (`DOOR3`, 72 tall; the synthetic `PANEL`, 72) shows "tutti-frutti" garbage or a wrong wrap when the wall is taller than it: reproduce that (behind a presentation option) or tile by the real size; (3) solid (one-sided, upper and lower) walls and the transparent pixels of a composite (`Textures.R_GenerateComposite` keeps holes; vanilla draws garbage there): a solid wall must not show the background through them, e.g. Doom II's missing pixels in `SKINEDGE` (73,36), `SKY2` (134,59 and 134,64) and `ZZZFACE3` (204,94) (T1.4a), so draw the stored index (or a fixed colour) and ignore the mask outside masked middles.

  *Note (from T2.4):* floors come from `FloorTriangles.Build(level, SubsectorPolygons.Build(level))`: per sector `BySector[s].Vertices` (fixed_t map x/y; the height is the sector's floor from the data texture) and `Indices` (triangle list), clockwise in map space with y up; check the front face after the axis flip (with x → +X, y → −Z they stay clockwise seen from above, which Godot treats as front-facing by default). Floor corners also sit in the middle of linedefs (seg splits, neighbours' corners), where a wall quad (per linedef side, corners only at the line's ends) meets the floor: a T-junction between the floor edge and the wall's bottom edge. If T2.9 shows cracks there, split wall quads at the floor corners on their line (the ring corners on the line between `V1` and `V2`).
  *Note (from T2.3):* use `WallSections.Build(level, textures)`. A section's quad runs `V1`→`V2` (its sector on the right) from `Bottom` to `Top`, both evaluated in the vertex shader from the per-sector data (`HigherFloor`/`LowerCeiling` are the max/min of the two sectors' planes, so masked middles need both sector ids). Clip upper, lower and middle sections to the front sector's floor–ceiling span and draw nothing where top ≤ bottom (`WallSection.Span` is the CPU reference); many two-sided sections have no height at load and only appear when a sector moves. Texture column `(TextureOffset + distance from V1) >> FRACBITS`, row `(TextureTop − z) >> FRACBITS`; a `MaskedMiddle` is drawn once (rows 0 to the texture height below `TextureTop`, `TilesVertically` is false). Texture 0 is never drawn (`-`; the synthetic IWAD's texture 0 is the dummy `NULLTEX`). The synthetic `E1M1` strip east of x = 448 (room C, closed door D, sky courtyard A, sky ledge B; see `SyntheticIwad.BuildMap`) has every pegging case for T2.6's side-on views.
  *Done:* `godot -- --level E1M1` (user argument read by `Main`; the WAD viewer stays the default) opens `scenes/Level.tscn` (`IsoDoom.Game.LevelScene`): `IsoDoom.Render.LevelMesh.Build` makes one `ArrayMesh` per sector (its floor triangles and the solid sections whose front sector it is; masked middles wait for M3, `-` sections are not drawn) with plane references, sector ids and texture slots in `CUSTOM0`–`CUSTOM2`/`UV`, a per-sector `RGBAF` data texture (floor, ceiling, light; `UpdateSectors()` re-uploads it), a texture-slot table and one RG8 atlas (`IsoDoom.Wad.Graphics.TextureAtlas`) of the level's wall textures and flats, all drawn by one material with `shaders/level.gdshader` (spatial variant of `palette.gdshader`: places vertices and computes wall rows from the planes, clips walls to the front sector, wraps texels itself, flat light `31 − light/8`). The fake contrast is per seg as in vanilla: `SideContrasts` splits a side into runs (DOOM1 E1M6 line 282 and 6 Doom II sides have an axis-aligned seg on a diagonal line), one wall quad per run, contrast in `CUSTOM2.z`. `shaders/level.gdshader` reads both tables from an R8 texture and computes everything after the float distance in integers. **Decision:** `LightDiminishing` presentation option (`--level-light=player|none|camera`, key L): `Player` (default; horizontal distance from the player, for now the free-fly pivot or player 1's start, `--level-light-origin=X,Y`), `None` (one reference distance, 256), `Camera` (view depth, vanilla's distance; exact vanilla for an eye-level camera). `extralight` and `colormap_override` (vanilla's `fixedcolormap`: 0/1, 32) are uniforms. `LightTablesTests` check table entries against linuxdoom's loops compiled as C (also screen size 9 low detail), the scale/z indexes, the clamps, contrast and the pinned seg-contrast sides. `--level-check` checks the table upload and every quad's contrast against its segs, and compares all rendered pixels with the CPU mapping (player distance; top-down also with none + extralight 1, camera depth, colormap 32), skipping pixels within 1/64 unit of a table step; at least two light levels and both contrasts are required.   *Done:* compared with **vanilla's own renderer, pixel by pixel**, instead of by eye: `tools/VanillaRef` (doomgeneric at a pinned commit plus `ref.patch` and a windowless `dump.c`; `build.sh`, `compare.sh`, `compare.py`; CLAUDE.md) renders vanilla's full-screen view from given points (eye level, no sprites, ceilings or sky, lights as in the lump) and the same views in the level scene offscreen (new `--level-script` commands `view X Y ANGLE` and `fov vanilla`, new `--level-background=RRGGBB`, `--level-light=camera`), and classifies every pixel. **E1M1:** 648 views (8 angles in every sector at least 56 units tall) plus 18 at the named spots (start room and its computer walls, the nukage zig-zag, the outside windows, the stairs and lift by the exit, the door tracks (lower unpegged), the secret door, the exit door): of 36.1 M drawn pixels 0.16 % differ beyond sub-texel sampling and vanilla's fixed-point wobble, 0.9 % by one light level either way (balanced: table steps at slightly different distances), close-ups of the doors are pixel-identical; sky lines as vanilla (no upper where both ceilings are sky); default light from above looked at in iso renders. **Bugs found and fixed:** (1) a **crack along the foot of walls** (E1M1 line 461 from sector 62: the node builder's rounded seg split vertex bends the floor 0.4 units off the linedef) and **slits where walls meet**: walls are now drawn **per seg on the floor's corners** (`IsoDoom.Map.WallPieces`, `FloorTriangles.SegChains`, connector pieces where floor edges don't meet; this also removes T2.4's floor/wall T-junctions); (2) **texture columns one texel off vanilla** where the node builder rounded seg offsets (E1M1 lines 127, 38): columns are now the seg offset plus the distance along the seg, as r_segs.c; (3) a sliver in Doom II MAP29's floor once seg ends were inserted: `WeldEpsilon` 1/32 → 1/8 and a fan fallback in the triangulation. Void pixels where vanilla drew all around fell from 7,759 to 5,213 (what is left is one-pixel ceiling strips that vanilla rounds to another row) and isolated void specks are 2 in 648 megapixel views; close-ups that were a texel off (`BROWN144`, line 127) now match exactly. **Every E1 map** swept the same way (4 angles per sector, 5,184 views, 298 M pixels; at vanilla's eye height, which stands on the highest floor within the player's radius): 0.05–0.16 % other per map, the light within one level as on E1M1; the remaining void pixels are ceiling-strip rounding and, in E1M4/E1M7, the missing (`-`) lowers (T2.3a), plus one dotted seam in E1M4 (T2.9b). **Load time:** `--level-check` now fails a map over 1 s (SPEC §9, the first map with opening the WAD) and prints the slowest; measured E1M1–E1M9 at 93, 47, 39, 31, 34, 42, 35, 20, 19 ms (editor, lavapipe; E1M1 first, with the JIT) plus 10 ms opening the WAD, Doom II at most about 100 ms. **Frame time** only under lavapipe (software): E1M1 at 1280×800 about 60 fps, at 1080p 18–27 fps; a GPU measurement is T2.9a. Tests: `WallPieceTests` (new), `FloorTriangleTests` (E1M1 now 726 triangles, 273 inserted corners); `--level-check` checks the pieces and takes the pixel compare's expected columns from them (still fails on a dropped texture offset). `dotnet test`, `--level-check` and `--viewer-check` (headless and windowed; synthetic IWAD, DOOM1, Doom II) and the export smoke test pass. See SPEC §12.
  *Note (from T2.9):* M3's masked middles (and anything else drawn along walls, e.g. the cutaway) should use `LevelMesh.Pieces` (per seg, on the floor's corners, vanilla's seg offsets) rather than the linedef's ends. `tools/VanillaRef/compare.sh` works for any map and IWAD (`-warp` from the map name); use it again when the renderer changes.

If it falls short, look at draw calls (one per sector chunk), overdraw and the fragment shader's table lookups.

## M3 — Iso camera + cutaway

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* a fixed iso camera follows a placeholder; walls are cut away; masked midtextures are drawn; things are drawn as billboards with rotations. There is no sim yet: the placeholder is moved by the presentation layer, and things stand in their spawn state.

*Order:* T3.1 and T3.2 are independent (T3.2 is plain C#) → T3.3 → T3.4 → T3.5 (needs T3.2 and T3.3) → T3.6 and T3.7 → T3.8. T3.1, T3.4 and T3.5 extend `--level-check` (CI on the synthetic IWAD).

**M3 is done (T3.8, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** On DOOM1 E1M1, E1M3 (dark) and E1M8 (tall) the orthographic game camera follows the placeholder, walls (and floors above the cutoff) are cut away around it, masked middles are drawn, and every thing stands as a billboard in its spawn frame with vanilla's rotation choice, sector light and full bright; what vanilla draws is unchanged (`tools/VanillaRef`, T3.8). Optional M3 tasks still open, none needed for the criterion: T3.4c (caps for solid areas outside sectors).

- [x] **T3.1 Masked middle textures.** Draw the `MaskedMiddle` sections T2.3 already builds (`LevelMesh` leaves them out now). Build them per seg from `LevelMesh.Pieces` (on the floor's corners, with vanilla's seg offsets; T2.9) rather than from the linedef's ends. Add a wall kind to `shaders/level.gdshader` that keeps the opacity mask (`discard` where G < 0.5) and clips to the texture's rows below `TextureTop` as `WallSection.Span` does (drawn once, no vertical tiling; r_segs.c `R_RenderMaskedSegRange`). Draw it in a second, alpha-scissor material or pass after the solid geometry. Light it as the other walls, with the fake contrast. Masked middles are two-sided, so check whether both faces must be drawn (vanilla draws the side the viewer faces; from above both sides of a grate can show) and log the choice in SPEC §12. SPEC §7.2.
  *Done:* masked middles are wall kind 2 in a second surface of each sector chunk with a second material, `shaders/level_masked.gdshader`; the shader code moved to `shaders/level.gdshaderinc`, shared with `level.gdshader`, and only the masked variant discards (`MASKED_PASS`), alpha scissor with depth writes. Quads per piece of the side (`LevelMesh.Pieces`), clipped to the opening and the texture's rows (drawn once, height from `texture_info`), columns wrapped as solid walls (`TextureWrap.MaskedTexel`, unit-tested), lit with `scalelight` and the fake contrast. **Faces:** one per sidedef, front face only, as vanilla (each side shows its own texture; both faces would z-fight with the other side's quad); one-sided masked middles stay invisible from behind (DOOM1: 1 of 91, Doom II v1.666: 64 of 430), T3.1a. `LevelMesh` sets every shader parameter on both materials; `MaskedQuads` counts the quads (DOOM1 E1M1: 13). `--level-check`: the masked surfaces' arrays and evaluated spans, the masked material's parameters, and side-on pixel compares of every axis-aligned masked middle over its whole opening (texels, holes and the rows outside the texture against the background; fails without the discard, the row clip or the contrast). `tools/VanillaRef` now draws masked middles (no sprites) and rebuilds itself when its patch changes; E1M1's fences match vanilla as the walls do. `dotnet test`, `--level-check` (headless: synthetic, DOOM1, Doom II; with lavapipe: synthetic, DOOM1 E1M1, Doom II MAP01, and the exported build on the synthetic IWAD), `--viewer-check` (headless) and the export smoke test pass. See SPEC §12.
  *Note (from T3.1):* the cutaway (T3.4) goes in `shaders/level.gdshaderinc`, so both passes get it; `LevelMesh.Materials` lists both materials. Masked middles write depth, so sprites (T3.5) sort against them by depth alone.

The sprite shader cuts a billboard as a wall through its foot facing the camera, by the walls' rule and style (cut/dither), at the point where the fragment writes its depth (the upright quad: the cut lines up with the walls' 32-unit stub on screen); a thing is cut only when its foot is in front of the centre's anchor (`Cutaway.HidesThing`, the CPU reference). `CutawayThings` decor (default) / all / off (`--level-cutaway-things`, key V; off = T3.5): **decor cuts every thing but actors** (`MF_SHOOTABLE`: monsters, barrels, the player; instance flag 4, `ThingSprites.FlagActor`), since with `all` the trooper in front of E1M3's placeholder became a pair of legs; E1M3's tech column is now cut down to a stub and the placeholder's legs show, and the hex room's armour (T3.4a's note) is cut with its pedestal. `--level-check` (real renderer, `CheckThingCutaway`): the tallest thing on its floor alone, with centres behind, behind and below, and in front of it, every drawn pixel classified on the CPU; cut as a decoration (also around the cursor centre) and as an actor with `all`, whole as an actor with `decor` and with things off, the dither clearing about half; it fails without the plane test, the cutoff, the actor flag, the cursor centre or with a 20 % larger radius. `dotnet test` (580), `--level-check` (headless: synthetic, DOOM1, Doom II; lavapipe: synthetic, DOOM1 E1M1, Doom II MAP01) pass. - [ ] **T3.4c Caps for solid areas outside sectors (optional).** T3.4a caps raised sectors only; inside a cut one-sided block (DOOM1 E1M1's computer pillars near (2300, −2320), thick walls) the void still shows (black by default, so it reads as a dark cap; the floor beyond can show through). Drawing one-sided walls' back faces dark below the cutoff would also put a stub on the outside of every room's near wall in the disc (the fixed camera culls those walls), so try the stencil buffer (Godot 4.5+ `stencil_mode`): cut fragments mark it, and back faces below the cutoff draw a cap only where it is marked (with a colour distinct from the background). Log the choice in SPEC §12.

## M4 — Sim core: movement

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* a 35 Hz tic loop; a twin-stick player with vanilla movement and collision (blockmap, step-up height, dropoffs); interpolation.

*Order:* T4.1 → T4.2 → T4.3 → T4.4 → T4.5 → T4.6 and T4.7 → T4.8 → T4.9. T4.1–T4.5 and T4.8 are plain C# in `Sim/`, tested on the synthetic maps in CI. Everything ported keeps vanilla names and cites its source file (CLAUDE.md).

**M4 is done (T4.9, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** The level scene runs the sim at 35 Hz (`TICRATE`) with one `ticcmd` per tic, independent of the frame rate (same checksums from 24 to 240 fps, T4.7/T4.9); the player moves with the twin-stick controls (keyboard and mouse, gamepad checked by script) and vanilla's movement and collision (blockmap, 24-unit step-up, dropoffs, sliding), matching vanilla tic for tic on every recorded route (T4.8, T4.8a); positions and angles are interpolated between tics without jitter; a tic takes under 0.1 ms on E1M9 with all things spawned (SPEC §9: 2 ms). The optional hands-on walk at real refresh rates (T4.9a) was done by the user on 2026-10-07: no jitter.

## M5 — Map specials

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* doors, lifts, switches, teleporters, light specials, damage floors and exits work; keys too; E1M1–E1M9 can be completed without monsters (`-nomonsters`).

*Order:* T5.1 → T5.2 → T5.3–T5.8 (any order; T5.6 needs T5.2) → T5.9 → T5.10. Each task adds its cases to a synthetic specials map in `tools/SyntheticIwad` (a new map, e.g. `E1M2`, so `E1M1` stays stable for the existing checks) and tests them in CI.

**M5 is done (T5.10, 2026-10-07): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** Doors, lifts, switches, teleporters, light specials, damage floors, exits and keys are ported from vanilla and tested on the synthetic specials map in CI (T5.1–T5.8). Every E1 map has a `-nomonsters` route from its start to its exit that matches vanilla on every tic (T5.9; E1M8's exit needs the barons' death, so its route starts in the arena). The same routes reach their exits in the level scene, also as one game through the whole episode (T5.10). A tic takes under 0.03 ms (p95) on the busiest map (SPEC §9: 2 ms). Optional M5 task still open, not needed for the criterion: T5.1a (render check on the synthetic E1M2). The hand play (T5.10a) was done by the user on 2026-10-07: nothing jerky.

- [x] **T5.1 Sector data from the sim.** Feed the sim's sector floor, ceiling and light values to `LevelMesh`'s data texture every tic, interpolated between tics for heights (lights step, as in vanilla), and per-side texture changes (switches, T5.4) through a per-side or per-section texture slot that can change at run time instead of a fixed vertex attribute. Decide how a changed texture that is not in the level's atlas is handled (switch textures are known at load from `P_InitSwitchList`; add them to the atlas up front) and log it. Port p_spec.c's lookup helpers (`getSide`, `getSector`, `twoSided`, `getNextSector`, `P_FindLowestFloorSurrounding`, `P_FindHighestFloorSurrounding`, `P_FindNextHighestFloor`, `P_FindLowestCeilingSurrounding`, `P_FindHighestCeilingSurrounding`, `P_FindSectorFromLineTag`, `P_FindMinSurroundingLight`) and `P_ChangeSector`/`PIT_ChangeSector` (p_map.c; things that no longer fit; crushing damage waits for M6's `P_DamageMobj`, so keep a hook).
  *Note (from T4.7):* the level scene runs the tics (`LevelScene.RunTics`/`Tic`) and draws at `TicFraction` (`PresentWorld`); sector heights need old values per sector as mobjs have (`P_StoreInterpolation` in `P_Ticker`, presentation-only and outside the checksum), then `LevelMesh.UpdateSectors` with the interpolated heights each frame. The world already changes the mesh's own `Level` sectors in place.
  *Done when:* moving a sector in the sim moves it on screen smoothly, `--level-check` checks a run-time texture change, and the helpers are unit-tested on the synthetic map.
  *Note (from T4.2):* the sim writes heights and light straight into the map's `Sector` objects (`sector_t` forwards to them), so the data texture can read `Level.Sectors` after each tic; keep the previous tic's heights for the interpolation.
  *Done:* **Heights:** `P_StoreInterpolation` also stores each sector's floor and ceiling (`sector_t.oldfloorheight`/`oldceilingheight`, outside the checksum); `LevelScene.PresentWorld` writes them interpolated at `TicFraction` every frame through `LevelMesh.UpdateSectors(heights)` (`InterpolatedHeights`), with the current light and floor flat; only changed texels are uploaded. **Run-time textures:** wall quads refer to their sidedef texture (`CUSTOM0.y` = 3 × side + part) whose slot is in the new `side_textures` data texture (-1 = none, the quad collapses); floors take their flat slot from the sector data's A; the sim changes `Side`/`Sector` names and `UpdateSectors` re-points the texels; a pegged texture bottom anchors on the slot's height in the shader (`WallSection.BottomPegged`, `TextureTopFor`). Every texture of a switch pair the level draws is in the atlas up front (`Sim/Switches.cs`: p_switch.c `alphSwitchList` per game mode; `LevelMesh.Build(textureGroups:)`, `LevelScene.SwitchGroups`); a name the atlas lacks keeps the old texture (`RuntimeMisses`, a warning). **Sim:** `side_t` and `World.sides`, `sector_t.lines`/`linecount`/`blockbox`, p_spec.c's helpers in `World.Spec.cs` (`P_FindNextHighestFloor` with Chocolate Doom's overrun emulation), p_map.c `P_ChangeSector`/`PIT_ChangeSector`/`P_ThingHeightClip` (`crushchange`, `nofit`; the damage goes to the `P_DamageMobj` stub). **Synthetic specials map:** the synthetic IWAD's `E1M2` (a corridor with 24 alcoves, tags, lights, the `SW1BRCOM`/`SW2BRCOM` pair; `SpecTests` test every helper, `P_ChangeSector`'s cases and the interpolation store on it). **Level check:** on every map, a sector moved in the sim during a tic is drawn at its interpolated heights (fractions 0, ½, 1) with its things kept on the floor, and a wall texture (its switch partner where it has one) and a floor flat are changed at run time and back (texels, GPU upload, chunk attributes evaluated with the new texture's height); with a renderer the changed wall is compared side-on (DOOM1 E1M1: SW1STRTN → SW2STRTN, 6820 pixels; synthetic E1M1: PANEL → BRICK1 on a pegged upper, 3500 pixels). **Debug:** the level script's `plane SECTOR floor|ceiling HEIGHT [SPEED]` and `smooth FRAMES SECTOR` (at 60 fps a 2-unit/tic plane steps exactly 1.17 units every frame). ## M6 — Monsters & weapons

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* info.c states, AI, all Doom 1 shareware weapons and monsters, pickups, damage, aim assist; the status bar HUD. The shareware IWAD has zombiemen (`POSS`), shotgun guys (`SPOS`), imps (`TROO`), demons and spectres (`SARG`) and barons (`BOSS`), and the fist, chainsaw, pistol, shotgun, chaingun and rocket launcher; cacodemons, lost souls, the Cyberdemon, the Spider Mastermind, the plasma rifle and the BFG are registered-only (M9).

*Order:* T6.1 → T6.2 and T6.3 → T6.4 → T6.5 → T6.6 → T6.7–T6.11 (any order) → T6.12 → T6.13. T6.10 can come first if sound events are needed earlier.

T3.8 kept it off; decide with aim assist whether aiming needs it and log it in SPEC §12.
  *Note (from T6.6):* the player's hitscans aim in p_pspr.c `P_BulletSlope` (`World.Pspr.cs`: the facing, then ±1<<26 with `P_AimLineAttack`, the slope kept in `bulletslope`) and fire along `mo.angle` in `P_GunShot`; `A_Punch` and `A_Saw` aim with `MELEERANGE` along `mo.angle` (and turn to what they hit). The snap can choose the angle `P_BulletSlope` starts from and `P_GunShot`/`P_LineAttack` fire along (e.g. by setting the angle for the shot only), leaving the functions vanilla; with vanilla's 7 shotgun pellets the spread stays around the snapped angle.

Off it returns `mo.angle` and traces nothing. On: when `P_AimLineAttack` along the facing finds a target the facing stays (the player aimed at something); else the angle to the centre of the nearest (`P_AproxDistance`, then smaller angle off, then thinker order) shootable, living non-player thing with some part in the cone (its centre within the cone widened by its radius's angle as seen from the player) and its edge within the attack's aim range, whose own aim line (`P_AimLineAttack` along that angle) finds a target, so things behind walls or out of vanilla's vertical window are skipped; the facing when none does. - [x] **T6.13b Door readability: lids (from T6.13a's hand play).** Found playing DOOM1 E1M1 at (1483, −2513), west of the door sector 4 (x 1536–1552, y −2560 to −2432, 16 units thick): **(1)** the cutaway (SPEC §7.3, §12 T3.4) cuts the door's camera-facing side (line 152) above 32 units inside its 80-unit disc whenever the player is behind that side's plane, so about half the 128-wide door disappears and the 32-unit stub left reads as a step; **(2)** even with `--level-cutaway=off` a closed door has no top (ceilings are never drawn, SPEC §7.2), so from above it is two 72-unit faces 16 units apart with a hollow slot between them, not a solid block that opens. **Fix (presentation only):** draw a **lid** on the solid volume above a sector whose ceiling is below a neighbour's (closed doors; the lintels over open doors and other low ceilings): a flat across the sector's floor polygons at the lowest neighbouring ceiling height, moving with the per-sector data (a door that opens leaves a lintel at the neighbours' height, so the lid's height may need the per-sector data texture to carry it; decide which neighbour heights count, e.g. sky-ceilinged neighbours). The cutaway cuts lids as it cuts raised floors, and T3.4a's cap draws a cut lid again at the cutoff (dark by default), so a cut door reads as a 32-unit block with a top. Decide what the lid shows (the sector's ceiling flat, the upper texture's top row, or a flat colour) and whether it is lit by the sector or its neighbours, and log the deviation from "ceilings are never drawn" in SPEC §12 (and §7.2/§7.3), behind a presentation option (`--level-door-lids=on|off`, a key). Look at it under the game camera on E1M1's doors (sector 4 above; the doors at (2912–2944, −3904 to −3776) and (2976–3040, −4648 to −4632)), a locked door (E1M2/E1M3) and Doom II MAP01, opening and closing, with the player walking through (the cutaway must still keep it visible: `tour`/`visible`). *Optional, separate if large:* highlight the line the use action would trigger (SPEC §12 T5.2's fallback), tinted by key colour for locked doors, as a presentation option (or under T8.5). *Done when:* closed doors under the game camera read as solid blocks with a top, cut or not; `--level-check` renders a lid (whole, cut with its cap, and on a door moved in the sim) and compares its pixels; all tests pass.
  *Done (2026-10-07):* **Lids** (`src/Render/DoorLids.cs`, plain C#, `DoorLidsTests`): a lid on every sector with a non-sky ceiling, a floor and a two-sided neighbour, at most 32 units thick (64 when closed at load: Doom II's thick doors), at the lowest neighbouring ceiling, drawn while the sector's ceiling is below it: closed doors, the 4-unit lintels over open ones, windows, thin low passages (611 of the 642 sectors closed at load in DOOM1 and Doom II; rooms with a low ceiling get none, they would be hidden). Its floor triangles once more per lid sector (vertex kind 5), placed from a new data texture `sector_lids` (lid height from the drawn ceilings, so it follows moving neighbours; the ceiling flat's slot); it shows the ceiling flat lit as a floor of the sector. Cut as floors are; the cap (T3.4a) also caps a lid's solid that spans the cutoff, with the lid's flat, so a cut door is a 32-unit block with a dark top. `--level-door-lids=on|off` (default on), key J, overlay line when off. **Looked at** under lavapipe with the game camera: E1M1's door sector 4 from both sides (closed, opening, the player in it, past it, closing), the doors at (2912–2944, −3904 to −3776) and (2976–3040, −4648 to −4632) from both sides, E1M2's red key door, Doom II MAP01's doors: they read as blocks with a top, cut or not. `tour 32` on E1M1 (654 steps) at least 81 % visible as with the lids off (91–93 % inside a door); MAP01 passes. `tools/VanillaRef/compare.sh` (four E1M1 views, two facing door 4) is identical with the lids on and off. **Level check:** every map's lid texels (also with a neighbouring ceiling moved) and lid vertices; with a real renderer E1M1 door 4's lid (synthetic: door D's) top-down and from the game camera 24 units behind it, whole, cut with its dark and flat cap, and with the door half open (ceiling above the cutoff), every compared pixel against the CPU; it fails with the lid 4 units off, the floor flat on the lid or its cap, or no cap. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1296, none skipped), `--level-check` headless (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic) and under lavapipe (DOOM1, synthetic) pass. SPEC §12 T6.13b. **Not done:** the optional use-line highlight (a T8.5 note).

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* menus, skill and episode select, intermission, finale, save/load, options, sound effects, OPL music, and the shareware download if cheap (T1.1b, SPEC §3).

*Order:* T7.1 first, then any order; T7.7 → T7.8 (T7.8a–g: see its order).

the lump list's kind column shows the rate, samples and length (or why DMX would not play it). **The sound table:** sounds.c `S_sfx` (`sfxinfo_t`: name, priority, `sfx_chgun`'s link to the pistol) in `src/Sim/Sounds.cs` (data only), `SoundInfo.LumpName` (a link plays its link's lump). **s_sound.c** in plain C# (`src/Audio/SSound.cs`, linked into the tests): 8 channels (`snd_channels`), `S_StartSound` (a link's volume, `S_AdjustSoundParams`' clipping at 1200 units, full volume within 200, the 1000-unit attenuation, map 8's whole-map hearing, `S_STEREO_SWING` separation; the listener's own and origin-less sounds in the centre at full volume; the origin's last sound stopped, `S_StopSound(NULL)` included, as vanilla's), `S_GetChannel`'s priorities, `S_UpdateSounds` every frame (channels follow their mobjs and the listener, stop out of hearing, free when done), `S_Start` at every level load, `S_PauseSound`/`S_ResumeSound` (vanilla's: the music only, T7.8), behind `ISoundDevice`; each channel counts its sound's length down itself, so the dummy audio driver (`--headless`) behaves as a real one. **The driver:** `src/Audio/SfxPlayer.cs`: an `AudioStreamPlayer` per channel on a bus of its own (`Sfx0`–`Sfx7`, an `AudioEffectPanner` each) sending to `Sfx`, Chocolate Doom's stereo law (half amplitude in the centre, `254 − sep` and `sep`) exactly. **The scene** (`LevelScene.Sound.cs`): the sim's events (`se_startsound` with its origin and position, `se_stopsound`) after each tic, the menus', intermission's and finale's through `IMenuHost.StartSound` (origin-less), `snd_SfxVolume = sfxVolume * 8` every frame (the `Sfx` bus keeps the options' volume), the listener's angle the camera's screen-up (`--level-sound-stereo=screen|facing|off`, default `screen`), the focus pause holding every channel, Quit waiting `I_WaitVBL(105)` (1.5 s, nothing running, no later menu sound cutting the quit sound), the overlay's `channels:` line. **Viewer:** selecting a digitized sound in the lump list plays it (double-click or Enter again; T1.6c's sound preview). **Level script:** `channels`, `playing NAME [ORIGIN]`, `heard NAME [MIN]`. **Checked:** `SSoundTests` (decoding and DMX's refusals, the table against `sfxenum_t`, every DOOM1 sound decodable, volume by distance, map 8, separation by side for a facing and for the game camera's screen-up, an origin's sound replacing its last, `S_StopSound(NULL)`, priorities with every channel busy, a channel following its mobj and stopping out of hearing, sounds ending, the focus pause, `S_Start`, Chocolate Doom's gains); `SoundRouteTests`: every route (synthetic, test maps, DOOM1) played through `SSound` a frame a tic with each sound as long as its lump: every start takes a channel at its tic unless out of hearing or every channel holds a better priority, at the volume for its distance and on the right side of the game camera's screen, and every removed mobj's sound stops (the event sequence itself is vanilla's, T6.10); `--viewer-check` (every playable `DS*` lump's preview stream is its samples at its rate; DOOM1: 55, synthetic: 1); `--level-check` (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic; headless): every table sound's stream, a sound 300 units west of the player at vanilla's volume on the left (player, bus, volume and pan), moved east to the right, stopped by its mobj's removal, a menu sound in the centre, the focus pause, the sounds ending, a level load stopping them; scripts: `route …/e1m1-weapons.route 300; tics 300; playing pistol player`, `route …/e1m1-exit.route; gamestate intermission; …; heard barexp; skip; map E1M2 1; heard sgcock`, the menu's Quit waiting for its sound. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1501, none skipped) pass. - [ ] **T7.7a Sound hand check (manual, for the user).** T7.7 ran headless (Godot's dummy audio driver) and scripted only: nobody listened. A person on a desktop (speakers and headphones) and on a Steam Deck: play DOOM1 E1M1–E1M3 (`godot -- -iwad wads/DOOM1.WAD`, New Game) and check that the sounds play at the right moments (weapons, doors, lifts, switches, pickups, monsters' sights, pains and deaths, the player's pain and death, the menus' clicks, the intermission's pistol ticks, explosions and shotgun cock, the quit sound before the window closes); that they come from the right side of the screen (a door opening up and to the left of the player is heard on the left) and fade with distance as expected; that the volume slider (Options → Sound Volume) changes them at once and 0 silences them; that losing the focus holds them and the game's pause lets them play out; no crackle, clipping or drop-outs with many sounds (E1M8's barons, a rocket into a crowd), and no stuck sounds after a reborn or a level change. Compare `--level-sound-stereo=facing` and `off` with the default `screen`; log the choice in SPEC §12. Listen to the viewer's lump list previews. Fix or add tasks.
- [ ] **T7.7b Sound options (optional).** The stereo mode (`--level-sound-stereo`) is a command-line argument only: add it to More Options (a Sound page, `Settings.Defs`, saved), with the number of channels (vanilla's `snd_channels`, 8; Chocolate Doom allows up to 16) if T7.7a finds 8 too few for the iso view, which hears more of the map at once than vanilla's eye-level view sees.

Create the licence notices (`THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` at the root, with Nuked-OPL3's LGPL-2.1 text and copyright; T8.7 finishes them) and keep the file headers. **Reference:** `tools/OplRef/` (a dev tool, never shipped, like `tools/VanillaRef`): `build.sh` fetches the pinned Nuked-OPL3 into `~/.cache/isodoom/opl-ref` and builds a small C harness that reads a register-write log (`SAMPLE REG VALUE` lines) and writes the samples; commit a few synthetic logs (a note per waveform, envelopes, vibrato/tremolo, OPL3 4-op and stereo, rhythm mode) and the reference samples' hashes. **Speed:** time a minute of OPL3 output at 49716 Hz resampled to 48 kHz (Release build): it must take well under 5% of a core on desktop (estimate the Deck from that, or measure it there in T8.1); if it is too slow, port DBOPL instead and log why in SPEC §12.
  *Done when:* the C# chip's samples equal the C reference's bit for bit on every committed log (`dotnet test`, the logs' samples or hashes committed; the test that needs the C harness skips without it), and the timing is logged in SPEC §12.
  *Done (2026-10-07):* **Pinned** Nuked-OPL3 `cfedb09` (2024-07-01, LGPL-2.1-or-later), the chip Chocolate Doom's master plays (its `opl/opl3.c` is Nuked-OPL3-fast 1.8-fast.3, bit-exact to `cfedb09`; Chocolate Doom 3.1.1 still vendors the 2018 v1.8, whose samples differ: SPEC §12 T7.8a). **Ported** in plain C# (`src/Audio/Opl3.cs`, linked into the tests, the file header kept): opl3.c whole with its names (`opl3_chip`, `opl3_slot`, `opl3_channel`, `opl3_writebuf`; `Opl3.OPL3_Reset`, `OPL3_WriteReg`, `OPL3_WriteRegBuffered`, `OPL3_Generate`, `OPL3_GenerateResampled`, `OPL3_GenerateStream` and the `4Ch` variants), integer-only, OPL2 and OPL3 modes, 4-op, rhythm, the write buffer and the linear resampler; the C's pointers are indices into `opl3_chip.sig`; two shortcuts with the same result for silent slots. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` created (Nuked-OPL3's copyright, commit and the LGPL-2.1 text). **Reference:** `tools/OplRef/` (dev tool): `build.sh` fetches Nuked-OPL3 `cfedb09` and Chocolate Doom's `opl/opl3.c` (at `895f581c`) into `~/.cache/isodoom/opl-ref` and builds `opl_ref.c` on each; `genlogs.py` writes the five committed logs (`logs/*.log`: waveforms, envelopes, vibtrem, opl3-4op, rhythm; 49716, 48000 and 44100 Hz, direct and buffered writes); `check.sh [--update]` renders them with both C builds (they agree) and checks `logs/SHA256SUMS`. **Checked:** `Opl3Tests`: every log's C# samples hash as the C's (always, CI too) and equal the harness's sample for sample when it is built (skips without it); a reset chip is silent and replays a log as a new one; the resampler's ratio at 44.1/48/49.716 kHz. **Timing** (Release, worst case: OPL3 mode, all 36 operators sounding with vibrato and tremolo, 48 kHz): a minute of output in 1.7–2.1 s, 2.9–3.6% of a core on the desktop (i7-11850H, shared with other builds; the C: 1.44–1.5 s), about 2.3% with half the channels, 1.7% silent: no DBOPL; the Deck estimated at 4–5% worst case (T8.1). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1571, 1 skipped: the worktree has no `wads/`) pass. The synthetic IWAD's `D_INTRO` is now a type 1 MIDI with a tempo track, running status, pans, bends, SysEx and percussion. **Reference:** `tools/OplRef/music_ref.c` links Chocolate Doom's own i_oplmusic.c, mus2mid.c, midifile.c, memio.c, opl.c, opl_sdl.c, opl_queue.c and opl3.c (fetched by `build.sh`) on a stub SDL (`stub/`), drives opl_sdl.c's mixing callback block by block and logs every chip write with its sample in opl_ref.c's format (so `check.sh` and `Opl3Tests` replay them too); `music.sh` writes the synthetic runs (`logs/music-*.log`, committed: D_E1M1 looping; volume, pause, resume, song changes, stop and `-reverse`; D_INTRO in OPL2 mode at 49716 Hz; the v1.666 drivers) and, for a WAD, every `D_*` lump's log to `~/.cache/isodoom/opl-music/`. **Checked:** `OplMusicTests`: the 5 synthetic runs write for write and sample for sample (SHA-256, always, CI too); every song of DOOM1.WAD (13) and doom2.wad (35), each whole and 10 s more (looping once), all at the same samples as Chocolate Doom's, and a minute of DOOM1's D_E1M1, D_INTRO, D_INTROA and Doom II's D_RUNNIN, D_DM2TTL bit for bit (48 kHz, 1024-sample blocks), 2.7 million writes in all; refusals and garbage, the volume, pause and `I_PlaySong`'s unpause, stop, looping, mus2mid's carried velocities, the float conversion, block sizes (a sample apart at most), through `IMusicDevice`. **Timing** (Release): a minute of DOOM1's D_E1M1 at 48 kHz in 1.6–1.7 s, 2.6–2.9% of a core. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` pass. SPEC §12 T7.8d. **Not done:** playback (T7.8e); the 60 s sample comparison is for 5 songs only (the others by their writes: the chip is exact, T7.8a); listening (T7.8f).
- [x] **T7.8e Music playback.** Play T7.8d's driver in Godot (`src/Audio/MusicPlayer.cs`, the `IMusicDevice` of T7.8c): an `AudioStreamGenerator` on the `Music` bus (T7.3's `LevelScene.MusicBus`; decide whether the options' volume is the bus's, as now, or i_oplmusic.c's `I_OPL_SetMusicVolume` as vanilla's (it changes the notes' levels, not the gain), and log it in SPEC §12) at the mix rate (44.1 or 48 kHz), fed from a dedicated thread that renders ahead into a small ring buffer (a few tens of ms; the sequencer's commands handed over lock-free or under a short lock), started and stopped with the scene, paused with the song. Headless (the dummy driver) the thread still renders at real time, so the tests and checks hear nothing but exercise it. The viewer's lump list plays a `D_*` lump when selected, as the sounds do (T7.7). The level check plays a song a few seconds headless (samples rendered, no buffer underrun counted) and the viewer check each `D_*`.
  *Done when:* the title loop, a new game and E1M1 play their music through the game (desktop, windowed), the underrun counter stays 0 over a 5-minute scripted run (`music` in the overlay with the buffer level and underruns), the render thread's cost is logged, and pause, focus loss, level changes, loading a game and quitting start and stop it without clicks or stuck notes.
  *Note (from T7.8c):* the device goes in at `LevelScene.InitSound` (`MusicDevice = new RecordingMusicDevice()`: make it the `MusicPlayer`, an `IMusicDevice`, `src/Audio/MusicDevice.cs`); `SSound` calls it from the game's thread, `I_RegisterSong` gets the lump's name and bytes (null back: silence), `I_PlaySong` must start the song unpaused (Chocolate Doom's OPL timer stays paused across songs; `SSound` assumes it does not), and pause/resume come only on a change (the game's pause and the focus pause together). `SSound.S_StopMusic` is the scene's quit (`QuitQuietly`); add the window's close. The overlay's `music:` line ends with the device's `ToString()`: put the buffer level and underruns there.
  *Note (from T7.8d):* `new OplMusic(genmidiLump, mixRate)` (OPL3; `opl3: false` for OPL2) is the device: it implements `IMusicDevice` itself, so `MusicPlayer` can forward the game thread's calls to it under one lock that the render thread also takes around each `OPL_Mix_Callback(span)` (it is not thread-safe: Chocolate Doom holds `OPL_Lock` and the queue's mutex the same way). `OPL_Mix_Callback` fills a stereo interleaved `short` span (overwrites it), running the song's events where they fall; its clock adds whole microseconds per run, so keep the block size steady (e.g. 512 or 1024 frames; any size works, timing moves by a sample at most). Make the instance at the mix rate (`AudioServer.GetMixRate()`); keep one for the game's life (mus2mid.c's statics carry over between songs, as Chocolate Doom's). `I_SetMusicVolume` changes the notes' levels (vanilla's; the volume starts at 0 until set, as the C's), so the `Music` bus could stay at unity; `SSound` already sends `musicVolume * 8`. `ToString()` gives a status for the overlay (mode, rate, playing/paused, voices, volume). A minute costs 2.6–2.9% of a core (Release, desktop).
  *Done (2026-10-07):* **Player** (`src/Audio/MusicPlayer.cs`, the game's `IMusicDevice` in `LevelScene.InitSound`, one for the game's life): T7.8d's `OplMusic` at the mix rate (OPL3) through an `AudioStreamGenerator` on the `Music` bus, fed by a dedicated thread that renders steady 512-frame blocks whenever the generator's ring buffer has room and pushes them itself (the game's frames never feed it); one lock over every forwarded call and each block; every call also recorded (`MusicPlayer.Record`, the checks' and the overlay's); no `GENMIDI`: silence. **Ring buffer:** 60 ms asked, at least two output-driver periods and two blocks (headless the dummy driver mixes 4096 frames at once: 371 ms there; 93 ms under PulseAudio); `--level-music-buffer=MS`. **Volume** (decided): vanilla's `I_OPL_SetMusicVolume` (the notes' levels), the `Music` bus at unity and muted at 0. **Stops:** the chip renders on, so pause, focus loss, level changes and loads are the driver's key-offs; every quit (the script's, the menu's Quit, the window's close, now handled by the scene, the checks') stops the song, fades 20 ms, waits for the buffer, stops the thread and frees the playback (no leak reported). **Overlay** `music:` (and the script's `music`): the driver, the buffer's level, underruns, seconds rendered, the render's share of a core (overall, now, worst block). **Level script:** `sleep SECONDS`, `underruns [MAX]`, `close`. **Viewer:** selecting a `D_*` lump plays it looping on the OPL player (double-click or Enter from the start). **Checked:** a 5-minute script (title, new game, pause, save, load, E1M2, E1M3, pause) with 0 underruns headless (300 s rendered) and offscreen under Xvfb + PulseAudio; `--level-check` (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic, a synthetic WAD without `GENMIDI`; headless, and offscreen with lavapipe for DOOM1 and synthetic): every map's song, the volume on the driver, the title's song played 3 s (rendered at the mix rate, peak heard, 0 underruns); `--viewer-check` (the same WADs; headless and offscreen): every `D_*` plays, one heard for a second. **Cost** (render under the lock): the exported release build 6–9% of a core (above T7.8d's 2.6–2.9% benchmark: a bursty thread), the editor's Debug build 26–50%. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1693) pass. SPEC §12 T7.8e. **Not done:** a person listening (T7.8f).
- [ ] **T7.8f Music hand check (manual, for the user).** On a desktop (speakers and headphones) and on a Steam Deck: play DOOM1 from the title through E1M1–E1M3 and listen: `D_E1M1` and the title music through a whole level without dropouts, crackle or stuck notes; the songs change at the title, each map, the intermission and the finale; the pause holds the music and lets the sound effects play out; the volume slider changes it at once and 0 silences it; compare a song with Chocolate Doom's OPL3 mode by ear. Fix or add tasks.
  *Note (from T7.8e):* listen for: the first ~90 ms after a song change, a pause or a level load (the ring buffer's latency: commands are heard one buffer later); a pause keys the melodic voices off and lets percussion ring (vanilla's DMX); a quit's 20 ms fade (no pop); the volume slider at 1 (vanilla's note levels are quiet low down) and 0 (muted by the bus); dropouts in a debug run (the editor) versus the export. The overlay (F3) shows `underruns` and the buffer's level; `--level-music-buffer=MS` if a machine drops out.
- [ ] **T7.8g Music options (optional).** OPL2/OPL3 mode and, if T7.8e found it useful, the ring buffer's size on More Options (a Sound page, with T7.7b's), saved; and SPEC §7.7's later General MIDI mode stays a separate task if wanted.
  *Note (from T7.8e):* the ring buffer is `MusicPlayer`'s `bufferSeconds` (`--level-music-buffer=MS`, 10–1000, default 60 ms, raised to two output-driver periods and two blocks); no machine here needed more, so an option is only worth it if T7.8f or T8.1 hear dropouts. OPL2 mode is `new OplMusic(bank, rate, opl3: false)`, made once in `MusicPlayer`'s constructor: switching needs a new driver (stop, re-register the song, play it again).

The cheats' messages go through `player_t.message`, which the message line already shows; `iddqd` needs `StStuff`'s god face (`CF_GODMODE`, ported).

## M8 — Shareware complete

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* all of E1 is playable from start to finish on desktop Linux and the Steam Deck; polish pass.

- [ ] **T8.1 Steam Deck.** Build and run on a Deck (native Linux build): gamepad layout and Steam Input defaults, UI and HUD scale at 1280×800, frame cap options (60/40/30, SPEC §9), suspend and resume, the IWAD search on the SD card (T1.1c). Measure frame time on all E1 maps.
  *Done when:* E1M1–E1M9 run at a locked 60 fps on the Deck, or follow-up tasks say what is short. Needs a person with a Deck.
  *Note (from T6.11):* the HUD's automatic scale at 1280×800 is 4 (the bar 128 pixels tall, the message font's capitals 28): judge it on the Deck's 7" screen (`--level-hud-scale`).
  *Note (from T7.8a):* time the OPL3 chip on the Deck: `dotnet test -c Release --filter-method IsoDoom.Tests.Audio.Opl3Tests.OneMinuteOfOpl3Output` prints its share of a core (worst case; 2.9–3.6% on the desktop, about 4–5% estimated for the Deck, SPEC §12 T7.8a). If the music thread's cost shows, port the rest of Nuked-OPL3-fast (Chocolate Doom's `opl/opl3.c`, bit-exact, about twice as fast as upstream; `tools/OplRef/check.sh` and `Opl3Tests` keep it exact).
  *Note (from T7.8e):* the Debug build (the editor's `godot`, F5) renders the OPL with an unoptimized JIT at 26–50% of a core (74% under lavapipe's load): no underrun here, but on a slower machine a debug run may drop out where the export does not; if it does, build the audio code in its own always-optimized assembly (`<Optimize>true</Optimize>`; `MethodImplOptions.AggressiveOptimization` does nothing in a debuggable assembly, tried) — T8.1 measures the export on the Deck (`underruns` and the overlay's `% now`).
- [ ] **T8.2 Gamepad aiming.** SPEC §13 Q5: right-stick aiming loses cursor distance. Tune aim assist strength on a pad, and try a lock-on toggle and a virtual cursor at a fixed or stick-scaled distance (for the camera's look-ahead). Log the result and close Q5.
  *Note (from T6.7):* the aim assist's strength is `Tweaks.AimAssistCone` (SPEC §12 T6.7), a sim setting every player of a game shares, like the skill; a stronger cone for a pad player only would have to move it into `player_t` (set at the game's start, part of the lockstep settings) or into the `ticcmd`. A lock-on can choose the absolute angle in `TiccmdBuilder` without touching the sim.
  *Done when:* E1 can be played comfortably with a pad (by a person) and the decision is logged.
- [ ] **T8.3 Performance pass.** Check every SPEC §9 target: 60+ fps at 1080p on integrated graphics for all E1 maps (with T2.9a's measurements), sim under 2 ms per tic on E1M9 with every monster awake, load under 1 s. Profile and fix what falls short (draw calls, overdraw, shader lookups, allocations per tic).
  *Done when:* the numbers are in SPEC §12 and meet §9, or have follow-up tasks.
  *Note (from T6.13):* the sim's tic time on E1M9 with every monster awake is far under the budget (0.13 ms median, 0.29 ms p95 in the release export; a deterministic run's worst tic 1.0 ms), but live runs show single tics of 2.1–3.8 ms at a different tic each run, with 4–6 garbage collections in 700 tics (`tictime` counts them): cut the allocations per tic and per frame (sim events, lists in the presentation), and check the spikes again in a release build on the Deck.
  *Note (from T4.9):* the first tics of a map took up to 6.6 ms in the game (JIT compiling the sim's paths; later tics under 0.2 ms on E1M9). Within a 60 fps frame, but on top of the first frames' rendering: look at it with `tictime` (SPEC §12 T4.9) on the Deck, and consider ReadyToRun or warming the sim up during the load if it shows as a hitch.
  *Note (from T6.9):* the fuzz pass (`shaders/sprite.gdshaderinc`, `FUZZ`) matches each fuzzed pixel's background to the palette by a 256-entry search; if it shows in a profile (many spectres up close), replace it with an inverse-palette lookup texture (RGB → index, one per palette, rebuilt with `SetPalette`).
- [ ] **T8.4 Windows and macOS builds.** Add a Windows export (and macOS, best-effort, SPEC §3) to `ci.yml` with smoke tests, and check the IWAD search there (T1.1c).
  *Done when:* CI produces and smoke-tests the Windows build.
- [ ] **T8.5 Readability and polish.** Look at the open presentation items with the whole game in place: tall rooms and the cutaway (SPEC §13 Q4: E1M8), camera smoothing and look-ahead feel, sprite readability (T3.6), muzzle flash, the optional enhanced lighting mode (SPEC §7.4: real Godot lights for flashes and projectiles, if cheap). Log the decisions and close Q4.
  *Done when:* each item is decided and logged.
  *Note (from T6.13b):* highlight the line the use action would trigger (SPEC §12 T5.2's fallback), tinted by key colour for locked doors, as a presentation option; T6.13b's lids made doors read as blocks, but which wall opens is still a guess from above.
  *Note (from T6.9):* look at partial invisibility in play: spectres are as hard to see over dark floors as vanilla's (a single colormap-6 darkening, streaks where it compounds), and the invisible player keeps only its solid outline (SPEC §12 T6.9); fuzzed things are not in each other's screen texture (one in front of another shows the background through it), and with `--level-sprite-hidden=upright` no sprite is (all are drawn in the transparent pass).
  *Note (from T6.12):* the iso death camera does nothing (SPEC §12 T6.12: vanilla turns the first-person view to the killer; here the corpse turns invisibly, its death frames having no rotations). Decide whether a dead player wants more: a pan towards or a marker on the killer, a "press use" hint (vanilla shows none).
- [ ] **T8.6 Regression routes in CI.** Keep the recorded routes (T4.8, T5.9, T6.13) as regression tests: run them through the sim and compare checksums with committed expected values. With `DOOM1.WAD` absent (CI), run the synthetic maps' routes; with it, run all.
  *Done when:* `dotnet test` runs the routes and a deliberate change to the sim fails them.
- [ ] **T8.7 Release packaging.** README for players (where the WAD goes, controls), licence notices (GPL-2.0-or-later, Nuked-OPL3's LGPL, any other dependency), a version number, and a release artifact from CI.
  *Done when:* a tagged build produces a downloadable Linux (and Windows) release.
- [ ] **T8.8 M8 acceptance.** Play E1M1–E1M9 start to finish on each skill on desktop Linux and the Steam Deck (people), including the secret level, saves, the intermission and the finale. Fix or add follow-up tasks.
  *Done when:* M8 is marked done here.

## M9 — Ultimate Doom

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* E2–E4, bosses and registered-only content. Needs a registered or Ultimate Doom IWAD (tests skip without it; add `ISODOOM_DOOM_WAD` as for Doom II).

- [ ] **T9.1 Registered IWAD checks.** Do T1.4b and T1.5b (textures and sprites), then load every map of E1–E4 in `--level-check` within the load budget, and run the IWAD search with an Ultimate Doom install (T1.1c).
  *Done when:* the Doom 1 full-IWAD tests and `--level-check` pass on the IWAD.
- [ ] **T9.2 Registered monsters.** Cacodemon (`A_HeadAttack`), lost soul (`A_SkullAttack`, the charge in `P_XYMovement`/`PIT_CheckThing`), Cyberdemon (`A_CyberAttack`, `A_Hoof`, `A_Metal`) and Spider Mastermind (`A_SPosAttack` reuse, `A_SpidRefire`), with their bosses' `A_BossDeath` cases (E2M8, E3M8, E4M6, E4M8; vanilla v1.9 rules, including Ultimate Doom's).
  *Done when:* each monster and boss ending matches vanilla on a route.
  *Note (from T4.4):* `P_ZMovement` reverses a charging lost soul's `momz` on hitting the floor *before* the landing zeroes it, as linuxdoom-1.10 and Ultimate/Final Doom's executables; Doom II v1.9's (and registered Doom 1.9's) does it *after*, so the soul bounces. Chocolate Doom picks by `gameversion` (`correct_lost_soul_bounce`); decide how the sim picks (game mode, or a setting) and log it.
  *Note (from T6.4):* `A_BossDeath` is ported whole (`World.Enemy.cs`, with Chocolate Doom's `CheckBossEnd`: `GameMode.retail` counts as The Ultimate Doom) and its cases are tested on `TestMap`s (`MonsterAiTests.TheBossSpecials`); `P_SpawnMissile` and `World.MissileSpeed` (the fast `MT_HEADSHOT`) are in. Check the episodes' boss specials against vanilla routes with a registered or Ultimate IWAD.
- [ ] **T9.3 Plasma rifle and BFG.** `A_FirePlasma`, `A_FireBFG`, `A_BFGSpray` (the tracers from the shooter's angle), `A_BFGsound`, and their pickups and ammo.
  *Done when:* both weapons match vanilla on a route.
- [ ] **T9.4 Episode flow and endings.** Episode select for registered (E1–E3) and Ultimate (E4, `retail`), secret exits (E2M9, E3M9, E4M9), par times (vanilla has none for E4), the intermission maps `WIMAP1`/`WIMAP2` with their animations (E4 uses `INTERPIC`), and the finales: E2 (`VICTORY2`), E3 (the bunny scroller `PFUB1`/`PFUB2` with `END0`–`END6`), E4 (`ENDPIC`), with their texts and music.
  *Done when:* each episode can be started from the menu and ends as vanilla.
  *Note (from T7.5):* the finale's texts and flats are in (`FFinale.textscreens`, every d_englsh.h text, by game mission), and `F_ArtScreenDrawer` draws `VICTORY2` and `ENDPIC` (`FFinale.ArtScreen`); E3's end picture is `PFUB2` drawn still: port `F_BunnyScroll` (`PFUB1`/`PFUB2` scrolling, `END0`–`END6`, `sfx_pistol`) and E3's `mus_bunny` at the stage change. Extend `finales.sh`/`FinaleTests` with registered/retail routes once a WAD for them can be tested.
- [ ] **T9.5 M9 acceptance.** Play E2–E4 through (people, or recorded routes checked against vanilla for each map), on desktop and the Deck, including frame time on the big maps.
  *Done when:* M9 is marked done here.
